Apache-2.0. Built on [Qwen/Qwen3.5-4B](https://huggingface.co/Qwen/Qwen3.5-4B) by the Qwen team (Alibaba Cloud),
Apache-2.0; served with unmodified [vLLM](https://github.com/vllm-project/vllm) (Apache-2.0).



================================================
FILE: assets.py
================================================
"""`assets`: images referenced by name anywhere in a request.

    "assets": {"<ref>": {"type": "image", "format": "png"|"jpg", "data_base64": "..."}
               | {"type": "image", "part": "<multipart file part name>"}}

The ref string is found and replaced wherever it occurs in the request's STRINGS: references are matched as plain
text anywhere in the request's strings, so a reference works mid-sentence as well as as a whole value. Whole values
match first. In longer strings, references are replaced from left to right, and matches never overlap. A short
reference such as `cat` could also match inside `category`, so use references that cannot occur in your text by
accident. Object keys are not replaced. So: plain substring matching, no word boundaries (a ref inside a longer word
IS replaced; that is the caller's contract), left to right, non-overlapping, the longest ref winning where two refs
start at the same place; keys never.
Backticks are NOT part of a reference: a question's backticked name refers to a STATE field ("the mug in `photo`",
where state.photo holds the ref), and stays text. Every asset must be referenced somewhere, and a multipart file part
must belong to exactly one asset; otherwise 400. PNG and JPEG only, 16 MiB per file, no URL fetching.

How it binds to the model: the shim's image path puts every image of a request ahead of the user text as image
parts and leaves `[image N]` placeholders in the text (lift_images). bind() turns each ref into exactly that: the
asset's data URI is appended to the request's image list ONCE per distinct reference, at its first use in order of
appearance (state, then questions in order: instructions, then criteria), and every use, whole-string or inside
text, becomes that reference's `[image N]` (N = its position in the top-level images list; images the caller
embedded in the state itself are numbered after the top-level list by lift_images, as before). Usage then counts
the image once.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import base64
import binascii
import re
from collections.abc import Mapping
from typing import Final

MAX_FILE_BYTES: Final = 16 * 1024 * 1024
FORMATS: Final = {"png": "image/png", "jpg": "image/jpeg", "jpeg": "image/jpeg"}
_PNG: Final = b"\x89PNG\r\n\x1a\n"
_JPEG: Final = b"\xff\xd8\xff"
PLACEHOLDER: Final = "[image {n}]"


class AssetError(ValueError):
    """A 400: the assets map, a reference or a part is not as the wire format says."""


def _sniff(raw: bytes) -> str | None:
    if raw.startswith(_PNG):
        return "image/png"
    if raw.startswith(_JPEG):
        return "image/jpeg"
    return None


def _decode(ref: str, entry: Mapping[str, object], parts: Mapping[str, bytes] | None) -> bytes:
    if not isinstance(entry, Mapping) or entry.get("type") != "image":
        raise AssetError(f"asset {ref!r} must be an object with type \"image\"")
    if "part" in entry:
        name = entry.get("part")
        if not isinstance(name, str) or not name:
            raise AssetError(f"asset {ref!r}: part must name a multipart file part")
        if parts is None or name not in parts:
            raise AssetError(f"asset {ref!r} names file part {name!r}, which the request does not carry")
        return parts[name]
    data = entry.get("data_base64")
    if not isinstance(data, str) or not data:
        raise AssetError(f"asset {ref!r} needs data_base64 or part")
    try:
        return base64.b64decode(data, validate=False)
    except (binascii.Error, ValueError) as error:
        raise AssetError(f"asset {ref!r}: data_base64 is not valid base64") from error


def resolve(assets: object, parts: Mapping[str, bytes] | None = None) -> dict[str, str]:
    """{ref: data URI} for the assets map, validated (type, format, PNG/JPEG bytes, 16 MiB per file, parts)."""
    if not isinstance(assets, Mapping):
        raise AssetError("assets must be an object mapping reference strings to image assets")
    resolved: dict[str, str] = {}
    used_parts: set[str] = set()
    for ref, entry in assets.items():
        if not isinstance(ref, str) or not ref.strip():
            raise AssetError("asset references must be non-empty strings")
        raw = _decode(ref, entry, parts)
        if len(raw) > MAX_FILE_BYTES:
            raise AssetError(f"asset {ref!r} exceeds 16 MiB")
        mime = _sniff(raw)
        declared = entry.get("format") if isinstance(entry, Mapping) else None
        if declared is not None and str(declared).lower() not in FORMATS:
            raise AssetError(f"asset {ref!r}: format must be png or jpg")
        if mime is None:
            raise AssetError(f"asset {ref!r} is not a PNG or JPEG image")
        if declared is not None and FORMATS[str(declared).lower()] != mime:
            raise AssetError(f"asset {ref!r}: the bytes are {mime}, not {declared}")
        if isinstance(entry, Mapping) and "part" in entry:
            if entry["part"] in used_parts:
                raise AssetError(f"file part {entry['part']!r} belongs to more than one asset")
            used_parts.add(str(entry["part"]))
        resolved[ref] = f"data:{mime};base64,{base64.b64encode(raw).decode('ascii')}"
    if parts:
        stray = sorted(set(parts) - used_parts)
        if stray:
            raise AssetError(f"file part(s) {stray} belong to no asset; each file part must be named by exactly one asset")
    return resolved


class _Binder:
    """Replaces refs in strings; counts uses; appends one image per distinct ref to `images`."""

    def __init__(self, resolved: Mapping[str, str], images: list[str]) -> None:
        self.resolved = resolved
        self.images = images
        self.uses: dict[str, int] = dict.fromkeys(resolved, 0)
        self.placeholder: dict[str, str] = {}  # ref -> its `[image N]`, set on the first use
        # longest refs first, so a ref that contains another is matched whole
        self.pattern = re.compile("|".join(re.escape(r) for r in sorted(resolved, key=len, reverse=True))) if resolved else None

    def text(self, s: str) -> str:
        """Every use, whole-string or inside text, becomes `[image N]`; each DISTINCT reference is bound once (its
        image appended to the top-level list on first use) and every later use points at the same placeholder (a
        reference used twice reaches the model once). One numbering for all uses; a whole-string data URI would be lifted and numbered a second time."""
        if self.pattern is None:
            return s

        def one(m: re.Match[str]) -> str:
            ref = m.group(0)
            self.uses[ref] += 1
            if ref not in self.placeholder:
                self.images.append(self.resolved[ref])
                self.placeholder[ref] = PLACEHOLDER.format(n=len(self.images))
            return self.placeholder[ref]

        return self.pattern.sub(one, s)

    def walk(self, value: object) -> object:
        if isinstance(value, str):
            return self.text(value)
        if isinstance(value, Mapping):
            return {k: self.walk(v) for k, v in value.items()}  # keys are never replaced
        if isinstance(value, list):
            return [self.walk(v) for v in value]
        return value


def bind(body: Mapping[str, object], parts: Mapping[str, bytes] | None = None) -> dict[str, object]:
    """The request with its assets bound: a copy whose state, questions (instructions, criteria) carry the images
    in place and whose top-level `images` list holds them (one entry per use, in order), `assets` and the parts
    removed. A request without `assets` is returned as is. Raises AssetError (a 400) on a malformed map, an
    unreferenced asset or a stray file part."""
    if "assets" not in body:
        if parts:
            raise AssetError("multipart file parts need an `assets` map naming them (asset: {'type': 'image', 'part': ...})")
        return dict(body)
    resolved: Final = resolve(body["assets"], parts)
    out: dict[str, object] = {k: v for k, v in body.items() if k != "assets"}
    have = out.get("images")
    images: list[str] = list(have) if isinstance(have, list) else ([have] if isinstance(have, str) else [])
    binder: Final = _Binder(resolved, images)
    if "state" in out:
        out["state"] = binder.walk(out["state"])
    if "input" in out:  # the OpenAI wire's state (a string, or user-message parts), before its questions
        out["input"] = binder.walk(out["input"])
    questions = out.get("questions")
    if isinstance(questions, Mapping):
        bound = {}
        for qid, q in questions.items():
            if isinstance(q, Mapping):
                q2 = dict(q)
                for field in ("instructions", "criteria"):
                    if field in q2:
                        q2[field] = binder.walk(q2[field])
                bound[qid] = q2
            else:
                bound[qid] = q
        out["questions"] = bound
    elif isinstance(questions, list):  # the OpenAI wire: a list of typed questions (choices/levels hold the text)
        bound_list = []
        for q in questions:
            if isinstance(q, Mapping):
                q2 = dict(q)
                for field in ("instructions", "choices", "levels", "criteria"):
                    if field in q2:
                        q2[field] = binder.walk(q2[field])
                bound_list.append(q2)
            else:
                bound_list.append(q)
        out["questions"] = bound_list
    unused: Final = [ref for ref, n in binder.uses.items() if n == 0]
    if unused:
        raise AssetError(f"asset(s) {unused} are not referenced anywhere in the request; every asset must be referenced")
    if images:
        out["images"] = images
    return out



================================================
FILE: box.py
================================================
"""The Box question: a bounding box for a referring phrase in an image.

Request: {"type": "box", "instructions": "the coffee mug in `photo`"} (no criteria; backticks name a state key that
holds an image; a request with no image is a 400). Answer: {"type": "box", "box": {"top", "bottom", "left", "right"}
as fractions 0-1 of the image measured from the top-left corner, "confidence": 0-1, "present": 0-1}.

Three stages behind one answer, all on the same engine:
  1. grounding: one base-mode chat generation (temperature 0, thinking off) asking for the target's box in the
     configured convention (for example "qwen1000": Qwen's own bbox_2d integers 0..1000; "gemma_y1x1": Gemma's
     box_2d, y first). The JSON in the reply is parsed, clamped and ordered. A reply without a box is "no box" (the
     full image with confidence 0).
  2. calibrated confidence: NOT the model's self-stated number. A decision readout on the image with the box drawn:
     a yes/no question "does the red box tightly contain <target>?", read from the one-token readout like every
     other question, with its own temperature (serve_config box.temperature; 1.0 until fitted). It estimates
     P(IoU >= 0.5). With box.confidence "generation" (serve_config's choice), the confidence comes instead from the
     one grounding generation's own token probabilities, calibrated by box.generation_calibration, and no drawn-box
     readout is made.
  3. presence: a yes/no readout on the untouched image, "is <target> in the image?", returned as `present`, so a
     caller can gate the box.

The referring phrase is passed through verbatim (relational targets among identical objects, region targets), never
reduced to a class name; only the readout questions strip the backticked image name.

The runner is pure: `run()` takes callables for the generation and the readout, so the shim wires its own vLLM client
and the fake engines in tests wire theirs.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import base64
import io
import json
import math
import re
from collections.abc import Callable, Mapping
from typing import Final

GENERIC_PROMPT: Final = (
    "{instructions}\n\nFind it in the image and return exactly one bounding box. Reply with JSON only, no explanation.\n"
    'Format: {{"left": L, "top": T, "right": R, "bottom": B}} where L, R are fractions (0 to 1) of the image width and '
    "T, B fractions of the image height, measured from the top-left corner."
)
QWEN_PROMPT: Final = (
    "{instructions}\n\nFind it in the image and return exactly one bounding box. Reply with JSON only, no explanation.\n"
    'Format: {{"bbox_2d": [x1, y1, x2, y2]}} where the coordinates are integers from 0 to 1000, relative to the image '
    "width and height."
)
GEMMA_Y_PROMPT: Final = (
    "{instructions}\n\nFind it in the image and return exactly one bounding box. Reply with JSON only, no explanation.\n"
    'Format: {{"box_2d": [y1, x1, y2, x2]}} where the coordinates are integers from 0 to 1000, relative to the image '
    "height and width."
)
# the grounding reply's JSON schema per convention (vLLM structured outputs through the OpenAI response_format
# json_schema: a short constrained decode, valid by construction, ints 0..1000)
SCHEMAS: Final = {
    key: {
        "type": "object",
        "properties": {name: {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "integer", "minimum": 0, "maximum": 1000}, "minItems": 4, "maxItems": 4}},
        "required": [name],
        "additionalProperties": False,
    }
    for key, name in (("qwen1000", "bbox_2d"), ("gemma_y1x1", "box_2d"))
}
GUIDED_MAX_TOKENS: Final = 32  # '{"bbox_2d": [1000, 1000, 1000, 1000]}' is ~20 tokens
# conventions: "qwen1000" (Qwen's bbox_2d x-first), "gemma_y1x1" (box_2d y-first),
# "fractions" (named 0..1 edges)
PROMPTS: Final = {"qwen1000": QWEN_PROMPT, "gemma_y1x1": GEMMA_Y_PROMPT, "fractions": GENERIC_PROMPT}
# the grounding call's system line (the decision engine's own system prompt asks for a single letter, which is wrong
# for a free JSON reply)
SYSTEM: Final = "You are a vision grounding engine. You locate the target named in the request in the image and reply with its bounding box as JSON."
SYSTEM_BY_CONVENTION: Final = {k: SYSTEM for k in PROMPTS}  # one system line for every family today; per-convention if a profile needs its own
DEFAULT_CONVENTION: Final = "qwen1000"
TIGHT_QUESTION: Final = "Does the red box drawn on the image tightly contain {target}, with the whole of it inside and little else?"
PRESENT_QUESTION: Final = "Is {target} visible in the image?"
_JSON: Final = re.compile(r"\{.*\}", re.S)
_BACKTICK: Final = re.compile(r"`([^`]+)`")
FULL: Final = {"left": 0.0, "top": 0.0, "right": 1.0, "bottom": 1.0}


# box.mode (each coordinate is the expected value of its edge's predicted position):
# "generate" = the constrained generation above (works out of the box on an untrained model);
# "edges" = four readouts over EDGE_BINS position bins per edge, coordinate = the expected value (a trained model's
# path); "hybrid" = both boxes formed, each read for tightness, the tighter one answered with its probability.
MODES: Final = ("generate", "edges", "hybrid")
HYBRID_THRESHOLD: Final = 0.7  # hybrid's early exit: a generated box this tight is answered without the edge path
EDGE_BINS: Final = 100
EDGE_QUESTIONS: Final = {
    "left": "Where is the left edge of {target}? Answer with its position across the image width as a percentage from the left border (0 = at the left border, 99 = at the right border).",
    "right": "Where is the right edge of {target}? Answer with its position across the image width as a percentage from the left border (0 = at the left border, 99 = at the right border).",
    "top": "Where is the top edge of {target}? Answer with its position down the image height as a percentage from the top border (0 = at the top border, 99 = at the bottom border).",
    "bottom": "Where is the bottom edge of {target}? Answer with its position down the image height as a percentage from the top border (0 = at the top border, 99 = at the bottom border).",
}
EDGE_ORDER: Final = ("left", "right", "top", "bottom")
# the two-level scheme for shims with fewer single-token labels than bins (the gemma profiles' 26 letters): a coarse
# readout over K equal bands, then a fine readout over K sub-bands inside the most probable band; resolution 1/K^2
_AXIS: Final = {"left": ("across the image width", "left"), "right": ("across the image width", "left"),
                "top": ("down the image height", "top"), "bottom": ("down the image height", "top")}
COARSE_QUESTION: Final = ("Where is the {edge} edge of {target}? Answer with the band it lies in, counting {k} equal bands {axis} "
                          "from the {origin} border (0 = the first band at the {origin} border, {last} = the last band).")
FINE_QUESTION: Final = ("The {edge} edge of {target} lies within band {band} of {k} {axis}, from {lo}% to {hi}% of the image measured from "
                        "the {origin} border. Where is it inside that band? Answer with the sub-band it lies in, counting {k} equal "
                        "sub-bands from the band's {origin} side (0 = the first sub-band, {last} = the last sub-band).")
MIN_EXTENT: Final = 0.005  # an edges box thinner than this is "not found"


def edge_options(bins: int = EDGE_BINS) -> dict[str, str]:
    """The choice criteria of an edge readout: option names "0".."bins-1", each described as its percentage."""
    return {str(i): f"{i}%" for i in range(bins)}


def expected_position(probabilities: list[float], bins: int = EDGE_BINS) -> float:
    """The expected value of the edge over the bins (bin i covers [i/bins, (i+1)/bins), its centre (i+0.5)/bins)."""
    if len(probabilities) != bins:
        raise ValueError(f"edge readout returned {len(probabilities)} probabilities for {bins} bins")
    return round(float(sum(p * (i + 0.5) / bins for i, p in enumerate(probabilities))), 6)


def band_options(k: int, fine: bool = False) -> dict[str, str]:
    """The choice criteria of a two-level readout: names "0".."k-1" described as band / sub-band i."""
    return {str(i): f"{'sub-band' if fine else 'band'} {i}" for i in range(k)}


def two_level_position(read_edge: Callable[[str, str, int], tuple[list[float], int]], image: str, edge: str, target: str, k: int) -> tuple[float, int]:
    """(the edge's position, prompt tokens): a coarse readout over k bands, the fine readout inside the most probable
    band, position = band/k + E[fine]/k (the fine expected value scaled into the band)."""
    axis, origin = _AXIS[edge]
    coarse, used = read_edge(image, COARSE_QUESTION.format(edge=edge, target=target, k=k, axis=axis, origin=origin, last=k - 1), k)
    if len(coarse) != k:
        raise ValueError(f"coarse edge readout returned {len(coarse)} probabilities for {k} bands")
    band = max(range(k), key=lambda i: coarse[i])
    lo, hi = round(100 * band / k, 1), round(100 * (band + 1) / k, 1)
    fine, n = read_edge(image, FINE_QUESTION.format(edge=edge, target=target, band=band, k=k, axis=axis, lo=lo, hi=hi, origin=origin, last=k - 1), k)
    used += n
    if len(fine) != k:
        raise ValueError(f"fine edge readout returned {len(fine)} probabilities for {k} sub-bands")
    return round(band / k + expected_position(fine, k) / k, 6), used


def edges_box(positions: Mapping[str, float]) -> dict[str, float] | None:
    """The box from four expected edge positions: clamped, ordered; None when it has no extent."""
    box = {k: min(1.0, max(0.0, float(positions[k]))) for k in EDGE_ORDER}
    if box["left"] > box["right"]:
        box["left"], box["right"] = box["right"], box["left"]
    if box["top"] > box["bottom"]:
        box["top"], box["bottom"] = box["bottom"], box["top"]
    if box["right"] - box["left"] < MIN_EXTENT or box["bottom"] - box["top"] < MIN_EXTENT:
        return None
    return box


# box.confidence
# "generation": the ONE guided generation is sent with per-position logprobs; each coordinate is the expected value
# over its digit tokens' distributions (place value combined), its confidence the chosen leading digits' probability
# (renormalised over digit-only alternatives, sharpened by `temperature`); no drawn-image tightness prefill, and the
# presence readout only when box.presence is on. Cost: one image prefill + ~20 decode steps.
CONFIDENCE_SOURCES: Final = ("tightness", "generation")


def calibrate(p: float, a: float = 1.0, b: float = 0.0) -> float:
    """Platt calibration of a confidence: sigmoid(a * logit(p) + b); (1, 0) is the identity. The generation confidence
    (a product of four leading-digit probabilities) runs low, and a temperature alone cannot shift it; the fitted (a, b)
    come from serve_config box.generation_calibration."""
    p = min(1.0 - 1e-6, max(1e-6, float(p)))
    z = math.log(p / (1.0 - p))
    return round(1.0 / (1.0 + math.exp(-(a * z + b))), 6)
_DIGITS: Final = re.compile(r"\d+")


def _ascii_digits(s: str) -> bool:
    """ASCII 0-9 only: str.isdigit() is true for superscripts and other Unicode digits a tokenizer may offer as
    alternatives (vLLM top_logprobs returned "\u00b9"), which int() refuses."""
    return bool(s) and all("0" <= ch <= "9" for ch in s)


def _number_spans(text: str, convention: str) -> list[tuple[int, int]]:
    """The character spans of the four coordinates in the reply, in the reply's order (x1,y1,x2,y2 or y1,x1,y2,x2)."""
    m = _JSON.search(text or "")
    if not m:
        return []
    key = "box_2d" if "box_2d" in m.group(0) and "bbox_2d" not in m.group(0) else "bbox_2d"
    k = m.group(0).find(key)
    if k < 0:
        return []
    start = m.start() + k + len(key)  # after the key (whose own digit, "2d", is not a coordinate)
    spans = [(start + n.start(), start + n.end()) for n in _DIGITS.finditer(m.group(0)[k + len(key):])]
    return spans[:4] if len(spans) >= 4 else []


def parse_box_logprobs(tokens: list[tuple[str, float, list[tuple[str, float]]]], convention: str, temperature: float = 1.0,
                       coordinates: str = "mode") -> tuple[dict[str, float] | None, float]:
    """(the box, confidence) from the generation's tokens. coordinates="mode" (default): the box is the generated
    text's own numbers (parse_box: the argmax decode); the per-digit
    expected value ("expected") would move the box: digit distributions are
    not position distributions, their mass on neighbouring digits shifts a box by whole digit places. The confidence
    comes from the distributions either way. Tokens:
    [(token_text, logprob, [(alternative_text, logprob), ...]), ...] as vLLM returns them with logprobs/top_logprobs.
    For each coordinate, every token covering its digits contributes E[chunk] over its digit-only alternatives of the
    same length (the chosen token included), combined by place value; a token that is not a whole digit chunk counts as
    certain. Confidence = the product over the four coordinates of the chosen LEADING chunk's probability (renormalised
    over digit-only alternatives, p^(1/T) with T = `temperature`): the model's own certainty about where each edge starts."""
    text = "".join(t for t, _, _ in tokens)
    spans = _number_spans(text, convention)
    if len(spans) != 4:
        return None, 0.0
    offsets, pos = [], 0
    for t, _, _ in tokens:
        offsets.append((pos, pos + len(t)))
        pos += len(t)
    values: list[float] = []
    leading: list[float] = []
    for a, b in spans:
        chunks = []  # (expected value of the chunk, digit length, chosen prob)
        for (ta, tb), (t, lp, alts) in zip(offsets, tokens):
            if tb <= a or ta >= b:
                continue
            piece = text[max(ta, a):min(tb, b)]
            n = len(piece)
            if _ascii_digits(t) and len(t) == n:  # a whole digit chunk: its alternatives are the same-length digit strings
                cands = {t: math.exp(lp / temperature)}
                for alt, alp in alts:
                    if _ascii_digits(alt) and len(alt) == n and alt not in cands:
                        cands[alt] = math.exp(alp / temperature)
                z = sum(cands.values())
                exp_v = sum(float(int(d)) * w for d, w in cands.items()) / z
                chunks.append((exp_v, n, cands[t] / z))
            else:
                chunks.append((float(int(piece)), n, 1.0))
        value = 0.0
        for i, (exp_v, n, _) in enumerate(chunks):
            value += exp_v * (10 ** sum(m for _, m, _ in chunks[i + 1:]))
        values.append(value)
        leading.append(chunks[0][2] if chunks else 1.0)
    conf = 1.0
    for p_ in leading:
        conf *= p_
    if coordinates == "mode":
        return parse_box(text, convention), round(conf, 6)
    scale = 1000.0 if max(values) > 1.0 else 1.0
    if convention == "gemma_y1x1":
        y1, x1, y2, x2 = values
    else:
        x1, y1, x2, y2 = values
    box = {"left": x1 / scale, "top": y1 / scale, "right": x2 / scale, "bottom": y2 / scale}
    box = {k: min(1.0, max(0.0, v)) for k, v in box.items()}
    if box["left"] > box["right"]:
        box["left"], box["right"] = box["right"], box["left"]
    if box["top"] > box["bottom"]:
        box["top"], box["bottom"] = box["bottom"], box["top"]
    if box["right"] - box["left"] <= 0 or box["bottom"] - box["top"] <= 0:
        return None, 0.0
    return {k: round(v, 6) for k, v in box.items()}, round(conf, 6)


class BoxError(ValueError):
    """A 400: the box question cannot be served as asked (no image in the request, bad instructions)."""


def target_text(instructions: object) -> str:
    """The target as plain text for the readout questions: the instructions with the image's backtick name removed
    ("the coffee mug in `photo`" -> "the coffee mug"); structured instructions are rendered as JSON."""
    text = instructions if isinstance(instructions, str) else json.dumps(instructions, ensure_ascii=False)
    text = re.sub(r"\s+in\s+`[^`]+`\s*$", "", text.strip())
    return _BACKTICK.sub(lambda m: m.group(1), text).strip() or "the target"


def parse_box(text: str, convention: str) -> dict[str, float] | None:
    """The box in a generation reply, as ordered, clamped fractions; None when there is none."""
    m = _JSON.search(text or "")
    if not m:
        return None
    try:
        obj = json.loads(m.group(0))
    except ValueError:
        return None
    try:
        if "bbox_2d" in obj or "box_2d" in obj:
            vals = [float(v) for v in (obj["bbox_2d"] if "bbox_2d" in obj else obj["box_2d"])]
            # box_2d is the y-first order [y1, x1, y2, x2]; bbox_2d is Qwen's x-first [x1, y1, x2, y2]
            x1, y1, x2, y2 = (vals[1], vals[0], vals[3], vals[2]) if "bbox_2d" not in obj else vals
            scale = 1000.0 if max(x1, y1, x2, y2) > 1.0 else 1.0  # integers 0..1000, or fractions whatever was asked
            box = {"left": x1 / scale, "top": y1 / scale, "right": x2 / scale, "bottom": y2 / scale}
        else:
            box = {k: float(obj[k]) for k in ("left", "top", "right", "bottom")}
    except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError):
        return None
    box = {k: min(1.0, max(0.0, v)) for k, v in box.items()}
    if box["left"] > box["right"]:
        box["left"], box["right"] = box["right"], box["left"]
    if box["top"] > box["bottom"]:
        box["top"], box["bottom"] = box["bottom"], box["top"]
    if box["right"] - box["left"] <= 0 or box["bottom"] - box["top"] <= 0:
        return None
    return box


def draw_box(data_uri: str, box: Mapping[str, float]) -> str | None:
    """The image with the box drawn in red (a 3 px line scaled to the image), as a PNG data URI; None when PIL cannot
    read it (the tightness readout is then skipped: a confidence about a box the model never saw is no confidence)."""
    try:
        from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
    except ImportError:  # pragma: no cover - Pillow is a declared dependency
        return None
    header, _, payload = data_uri.partition(",")
    try:
        image = Image.open(io.BytesIO(base64.b64decode(payload))).convert("RGB")
    except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - not an image PIL reads
        return None
    w, h = image.size
    draw = ImageDraw.Draw(image)
    width = max(2, round(min(w, h) / 200))
    draw.rectangle([box["left"] * w, box["top"] * h, box["right"] * w, box["bottom"] * h], outline=(255, 0, 0), width=width)
    out = io.BytesIO()
    image.save(out, format="PNG")
    return "data:image/png;base64," + base64.b64encode(out.getvalue()).decode("ascii")


def image_for(question: Mapping[str, object], state: object, images: list[str]) -> int:
    """Which of the request's images the question targets: the one a backtick name in the instructions points at
    (a state key holding an `[image N]` placeholder), else the first image."""
    ins = question.get("instructions")
    if isinstance(ins, str) and isinstance(state, Mapping):
        for name in _BACKTICK.findall(ins):
            value = state.get(name)
            m = re.fullmatch(r"\[image (\d+)\]", str(value).strip()) if value is not None else None
            if m and 1 <= int(m.group(1)) <= len(images):
                return int(m.group(1)) - 1
    return 0


def run(
    question: Mapping[str, object],
    state: object,
    images: list[str],
    *,
    generate: Callable[[str, str], tuple[str, int]],
    read_noul: Callable[[str, str], tuple[float, int]],
    convention: str = DEFAULT_CONVENTION,
    mode: str = "generate",
    read_edge: Callable[[str, str, int], tuple[list[float], int]] | None = None,
    bins: int = EDGE_BINS,
    two_level: int | None = None,
    submit: Callable[..., object] | None = None,
    hybrid_threshold: float = HYBRID_THRESHOLD,
    confidence: str = "tightness",
    presence: bool = True,
    generation_temperature: float = 1.0,
    generation_calibration: tuple[float, float] = (1.0, 0.0),
) -> tuple[dict[str, object], int]:
    """(the answer, input tokens used). `generate(image_uri, prompt) -> (text, prompt_tokens)`;
    `read_noul(image_uri, question_text) -> (p_yes, prompt_tokens)`;
    `read_edge(image_uri, question_text, n) -> (probabilities over the n options, prompt_tokens)` (modes edges/hybrid):
    flat over `bins` options, or, with `two_level=K`, a coarse + fine readout over K bands each (the shim's option
    count, e.g. 26) â€” the caller's shim renders the options in the order given, never permuted (ordinal labels).
    `submit(fn, *args) -> future`: with it, the independent calls of a box (the grounding generation, the four edge
    readouts, the presence readout) run CONCURRENTLY on the same engine, sharing the image's prefix cache (an edges box
    should cost about one or two yes/no latencies, not six); only the tightness readouts
    wait for their boxes. Without it, everything runs in order (tests, package shims without a pool)."""
    if not images:
        raise BoxError("a box question needs an image in the request (an image in the state, an asset, or images)")
    if convention not in PROMPTS:
        raise BoxError(f"unknown box convention {convention!r}")
    if mode not in MODES:
        raise BoxError(f"unknown box mode {mode!r}")
    if confidence not in CONFIDENCE_SOURCES:
        raise BoxError(f"unknown box confidence source {confidence!r}")
    if mode != "generate" and read_edge is None:
        raise BoxError(f"box mode {mode!r} needs an edge readout")
    target: Final = target_text(question.get("instructions"))
    image: Final = images[image_for(question, state, images)]

    class _Now:  # the sequential stand-in for a future
        def __init__(self, fn, *args):
            self.value = fn(*args)

        def result(self):
            return self.value

    start = submit if submit is not None else _Now
    used = 0
    jobs: dict[str, object] = {}
    tight_q: Final = TIGHT_QUESTION.format(target=target)

    def start_edges() -> None:
        for edge in EDGE_ORDER:
            if two_level:
                jobs[edge] = start(two_level_position, read_edge, image, edge, target, two_level)
            else:
                jobs[edge] = start(read_edge, image, EDGE_QUESTIONS[edge].format(target=target), bins)

    if mode in ("generate", "hybrid"):
        jobs["generate"] = start(generate, image, PROMPTS[convention].format(instructions=str(question.get("instructions") or target)))
    if mode == "edges":
        start_edges()
    if presence:
        jobs["present"] = start(read_noul, image, PRESENT_QUESTION.format(target=target))
    candidates: list[dict[str, float]] = []
    if "generate" in jobs:
        got = jobs["generate"].result()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
        text, n = got[0], got[1]
        used += n
        if confidence == "generation" and mode == "generate":
            # the generation's own distributions: expected-value box and its confidence, no tightness prefill
            lp_tokens = got[2] if len(got) > 2 else None
            box, conf = parse_box_logprobs(lp_tokens, convention, generation_temperature) if lp_tokens else (parse_box(text, convention), 0.0)
            if lp_tokens and box is not None:
                conf = calibrate(conf, *generation_calibration)  # the fitted Platt map (serve_config box.generation_calibration)
            present_v = jobs["present"].result() if "present" in jobs else None  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
            if present_v is not None:
                used += present_v[1]
            answer: dict[str, object] = {"type": "box", "box": box if box is not None else dict(FULL), "confidence": conf if box is not None else 0.0,
                                         "found": box is not None}
            # `present` keeps its place and meaning in the answer (never dropped): the presence
            # readout when box.presence is on; with it off, the generation's own evidence that the target was located:
            # the calibrated box confidence (a located box with a confident grounding), 0.0 when no box came back
            answer["present"] = present_v[0] if present_v is not None else (answer["confidence"] if box is not None else 0.0)
            return answer, used
        box = parse_box(text, convention)
        if box is not None:
            candidates.append(box)
    if mode == "hybrid":
        # the early exit: the generated box is read for tightness first; when it is tight
        # enough the edge path never runs, so the common case costs what generate costs
        drawn = draw_box(image, candidates[0]) if candidates else None
        if drawn is not None:
            tight, n = read_noul(drawn, tight_q)
            used += n
            if tight >= hybrid_threshold:
                out_h: dict[str, object] = {"type": "box", "box": candidates[0], "confidence": tight, "found": True}
                if "present" in jobs:
                    present, n = jobs["present"].result()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
                    used += n
                    out_h["present"] = present
                return out_h, used
            jobs["tight:0"] = _Now(lambda: (tight, 0))  # already read: reused below, not read again
        start_edges()
    if mode in ("edges", "hybrid"):
        positions = {}
        for edge in EDGE_ORDER:
            value, n = jobs[edge].result()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
            positions[edge] = value if two_level else expected_position(value, bins)
            used += n
        box = edges_box(positions)
        if box is not None:
            candidates.append(box)
    present = None
    if "present" in jobs:
        present, n = jobs["present"].result()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
        used += n
    if not candidates:
        out: dict[str, object] = {"type": "box", "box": dict(FULL), "confidence": 0.0, "found": False}
        if present is not None:
            out["present"] = present
        return out, used
    tight_jobs = []  # hybrid: both candidates read at once; the tighter by its own readout wins
    for i, box in enumerate(candidates):
        if f"tight:{i}" in jobs:  # hybrid's early-exit readout, already taken
            tight_jobs.append((box, jobs[f"tight:{i}"]))
            continue
        drawn = draw_box(image, box)
        tight_jobs.append((box, None if drawn is None else start(read_noul, drawn, tight_q)))
    best: dict[str, float] | None = None
    best_tight = -1.0
    for box, job in tight_jobs:
        if job is None:  # the box could not be drawn: found, but no readout to back a confidence
            tight = 0.0
        else:
            tight, n = job.result()  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
            used += n
        if tight > best_tight:
            best, best_tight = box, tight
    final: dict[str, object] = {"type": "box", "box": best, "confidence": max(0.0, best_tight), "found": True}
    if present is not None:
        final["present"] = present
    return final, used



================================================
FILE: card_common.py
================================================
"""Helpers for the model-card conformance tests (test_card.py): the card is parsed, its commands are run and its
examples are sent as written, so the card and the tests cannot drift apart.

Inputs (environment):
  CARD_REPO   the Hub repo whose card is tested (default h2oai/h2o-lightning-4b)
  CARD_REV    the exact 40-hex commit under test (required unless CARD_DIR is given)
  CARD_DIR    a folder that already holds that commit's files (optional; else a fresh snapshot is downloaded)
  CARD_LIVE   1 to run the live tier (a GPU, vLLM 0.30.0 and the card's launch commands)
  CARD_REPORT where the JSON report goes (default card_report.json in the working directory)
Tiers are pytest markers: static (CPU, seconds, the default), live (serves the model), slow (recomputes numbers).
Nothing here writes to the Hub."""
import atexit
import hashlib
import json
import os
import re
import subprocess
import time
import urllib.request

DEFAULT_REPO = "h2oai/h2o-lightning-4b"
HEX40 = re.compile(r"^[0-9a-f]{40}$")


def env(name, default=None):
    v = os.environ.get(name)
    return v if v not in (None, "") else default


def sha256_file(path):
    h = hashlib.sha256()
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        for c in iter(lambda: f.read(1 << 24), b""):
            h.update(c)
    return h.hexdigest()


class Report:
    """Collects what the tests read and found; written as JSON when the interpreter exits."""

    def __init__(self):
        self.data = {"repo": None, "revision": None, "snapshot_commit": None, "files_read": {}, "results": {}, "notes": []}
        atexit.register(self.write)

    def read(self, path):
        p = os.path.abspath(path)
        if p not in self.data["files_read"] and os.path.isfile(p):
            self.data["files_read"][p] = sha256_file(p)
        return p

    def result(self, name, value):
        self.data["results"][name] = value

    def note(self, text):
        self.data["notes"].append(text)

    def write(self):
        with open(env("CARD_REPORT", "card_report.json"), "w") as f:
            json.dump(self.data, f, indent=1, sort_keys=True)


REPORT = Report()


def fresh_snapshot(repo, rev, ignore_patterns=None, token=None):
    """The exact commit's files: CARD_DIR when given (its README must be that commit's), else a snapshot into a fresh
    HF_HOME under the working directory; returns (folder, commit sha the Hub reported). ignore_patterns leaves out
    files the static tier does not read (the GGUF weights); token=False proves a public repo needs no login."""
    d = env("CARD_DIR")
    if d:
        REPORT.data.update(repo=repo, revision=rev, snapshot_commit=rev)
        return os.path.abspath(d), rev
    if not (rev and HEX40.match(rev)):
        raise RuntimeError("CARD_REV must be the full 40-hex commit under test")
    from huggingface_hub import HfApi, snapshot_download
    home = os.path.abspath(f"card_snapshot_{rev[:12]}")
    os.environ["HF_HOME"] = os.path.join(home, "hf_home")
    folder = snapshot_download(repo, revision=rev, local_dir=os.path.join(home, "files"), ignore_patterns=ignore_patterns,
                               token=token)
    got = HfApi().model_info(repo, revision=rev, token=token).sha
    REPORT.data.update(repo=repo, revision=rev, snapshot_commit=got)
    return folder, got


def readme_blocks(text):
    """Every fenced block of the card: {"index", "lang", "code", "heading"} with the nearest heading above it."""
    out, heading = [], ""
    for m in re.finditer(r"^(#{1,6})[ \t]+([^\n]*)$|```(\w*)\n(.*?)```", text, re.S | re.M):
        if m.group(1):
            heading = m.group(2).strip()
        else:
            out.append({"index": len(out), "lang": m.group(3) or "", "code": m.group(4), "heading": heading, "pos": m.start()})
    return out


def referenced_paths(text):
    """File names the card mentions in backticks that look like package files."""
    names = set(re.findall(r"`([\w./-]+\.(?:py|json|sh|jinja|lightning|safetensors|md|txt))`", text))
    return sorted(n.split("/")[-1] if n.startswith("h2o-lightning-4b/") else n for n in names)


def claims_check(text, claims):
    """Each registered claim quote must appear verbatim in the card; returns the missing ones."""
    return [c["quote"] for c in claims if c["quote"] not in text]


NUMBER = re.compile(r"\d")


def numeric_sentences(text, headings):
    """Sentences with a number under the given headings (prose only, fenced blocks and tables excluded)."""
    out = []
    section = None
    for line in text.splitlines():
        if line.startswith("#"):
            section = line.lstrip("#").strip()
            continue
        if section in headings and not line.startswith(("|", "```", ">", "    ")) and NUMBER.search(line):
            out.append((section, line.strip()))
    return out


def substitute(code, mapping):
    for k, v in mapping.items():
        code = code.replace(k, v)
    return code


def run_card_command(code, cwd, log_path, extra_env=None):
    """Runs a bash block as written (after substitutions) in the background; returns the Popen."""
    e = dict(os.environ)
    e.update(extra_env or {})
    log = open(log_path, "ab")
    return subprocess.Popen(["bash", "-c", code], cwd=cwd, env=e, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, start_new_session=True)


def wait_http(url, seconds, proc=None):
    """Until the URL answers 200; a process that exits first is a failure."""
    t0 = time.time()
    while time.time() - t0 < seconds:
        if proc is not None and proc.poll() is not None:
            return False
        try:
            with urllib.request.urlopen(url, timeout=5) as r:
                if r.status == 200:
                    return True
        except Exception:
            pass
        time.sleep(3)
    return False


def wait_log(path, needle, seconds, proc=None):
    t0 = time.time()
    while time.time() - t0 < seconds:
        if os.path.isfile(path) and needle in open(path, "rb").read().decode("utf-8", "replace"):
            return True
        if proc is not None and proc.poll() is not None:
            return False
        time.sleep(3)
    return False


def stop_tree(proc):
    """Stops the process group a card command started (its vLLM and shim children included)."""
    import signal
    if proc is None or proc.poll() is not None:
        return
    try:
        os.killpg(os.getpgid(proc.pid), signal.SIGTERM)
    except ProcessLookupError:
        return
    for _ in range(40):
        if proc.poll() is not None:
            break
        time.sleep(1)
    if proc.poll() is None:
        os.killpg(os.getpgid(proc.pid), signal.SIGKILL)
    time.sleep(5)


def post_example(url, body, timeout=900):
    req = urllib.request.Request(url, data=json.dumps(body).encode(), headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    try:
        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as r:
            return r.status, json.loads(r.read().decode())
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as e:
        raw = e.read().decode("utf-8", "replace")
        try:
            return e.code, json.loads(raw)
        except ValueError:
            return e.code, {"raw": raw[:500]}


def curl_json_bodies(code):
    """The JSON bodies of the curl commands in a bash block (the -d '...' arguments), parsed; and their URLs."""
    out = []
    for m in re.finditer(r"curl\s+(.*?)-d\s+'(.*?)'", code, re.S):
        url = re.search(r"https?://[^\s'\"]+", m.group(1))
        body = m.group(2)
        try:
            out.append((url.group(0) if url else None, json.loads(body)))
        except ValueError:
            pass
    return out


def safetensors_keys(path):
    import struct
    with open(path, "rb") as f:
        n = struct.unpack("<Q", f.read(8))[0]
        h = json.loads(f.read(n))
    meta = h.pop("__metadata__", None)
    return h, meta


def weights_differ_from_base(folder, base_repo, base_rev, max_bytes=1 << 20, tensor="q_proj.weight"):
    """One tensor of the shipped weights against the same tensor of the base model on the Hub (range read): the
    fraction of equal bf16 values must be below 0.5, so the shipped file is not the base under another name.
    The base may be one model.safetensors or sharded (its index names the shard)."""
    import struct
    hdr, _ = safetensors_keys(os.path.join(folder, "model.safetensors"))
    name = sorted(k for k in hdr if k.endswith(tensor))[0]
    ours = hdr[name]
    with open(os.path.join(folder, "model.safetensors"), "rb") as f:
        n = struct.unpack("<Q", f.read(8))[0]
        f.seek(8 + n + ours["data_offsets"][0])
        a = f.read(min(max_bytes, ours["data_offsets"][1] - ours["data_offsets"][0]))
    base_name, b = hub_safetensors_head(base_repo, base_rev, name, len(a), fallback=name.replace("model.language_model.", "model."))
    return name, bf16_equal_share(a, b[:len(a)])


# --- additions for the GGUF and Nemotron-Nano cards (range reads, claim registry, example shapes) ---

def hub_safetensors_head(repo, rev, tensor, n=1 << 20, fallback=None):
    """First n bytes of one tensor of a Hub safetensors model (single file or sharded), by range reads only.
    `tensor` may be a full name or a suffix; `fallback` is tried when the name is not in the model."""
    import struct
    from huggingface_hub import HfFileSystem, get_safetensors_metadata
    m = get_safetensors_metadata(repo, revision=rev, token=False)
    names = [tensor, fallback] if fallback else [tensor]
    name = next((x for x in names if x in m.weight_map), None) or next(k for k in m.weight_map if k.endswith(tensor))
    fname = m.weight_map[name]
    with HfFileSystem(token=False).open(f"{repo}@{rev}/{fname}", "rb", block_size=n) as f:
        hlen = struct.unpack("<Q", f.read(8))[0]
        f.seek(8 + hlen + m.files_metadata[fname].tensors[name].data_offsets[0])
        return name, f.read(n)


def hub_gguf_head(repo, rev, fname, tensor, n=1 << 20):
    """First n bytes of one tensor of a Hub GGUF file (v2/v3), by range reads; returns (dims, ggml type, bytes)."""
    import struct
    from huggingface_hub import HfFileSystem
    sizes = {0: 1, 1: 1, 2: 2, 3: 2, 4: 4, 5: 4, 6: 4, 7: 1, 10: 8, 11: 8, 12: 8}
    with HfFileSystem(token=False).open(f"{repo}@{rev}/{fname}", "rb", block_size=16 << 20) as f:
        u32 = lambda: struct.unpack("<I", f.read(4))[0]
        u64 = lambda: struct.unpack("<Q", f.read(8))[0]
        s = lambda: f.read(u64()).decode("utf-8", "replace")

        def val(t):
            if t == 8:
                return s()
            if t == 9:
                et, k = u32(), u64()
                return [val(et) for _ in range(k)]
            return f.read(sizes[t])

        if f.read(4) != b"GGUF":
            raise ValueError(f"{fname} is not GGUF")
        u32()
        nt, nkv = u64(), u64()
        align = 32
        for _ in range(nkv):
            k, t = s(), u32()
            v = val(t)
            if k == "general.alignment":
                align = struct.unpack("<I", v)[0]
        infos = {}
        for _ in range(nt):
            name, nd = s(), u32()
            dims = [u64() for _ in range(nd)]
            infos[name] = (dims, u32(), u64())
        base = (f.tell() + align - 1) // align * align
        dims, ty, off = infos[tensor]
        f.seek(base + off)
        return dims, ty, f.read(n)


def bf16_equal_share(a, b):
    """Share of equal 16-bit values in two equal-length byte strings."""
    n = min(len(a), len(b)) // 2
    return sum(a[2 * i:2 * i + 2] == b[2 * i:2 * i + 2] for i in range(n)) / max(1, n)


NUMERIC_CLAIM = re.compile(r"\d+\.\d+|\d+(?:\.\d+)?\s?%|\b\d+\s?/\s?\d+\b|"
                           r"\b\d[\d,]*\s?(?:GB|MB|MiB|GiB|ms|s|tokens|questions|items)\b")


def prose_lines(text):
    """(line number, line) of the card outside the front matter and fenced blocks."""
    out, fenced, start = [], False, 0
    lines = text.splitlines()
    if lines and lines[0] == "---":
        start = lines.index("---", 1) + 1
    for i, line in enumerate(lines[start:], start=start + 1):
        if line.startswith("```"):
            fenced = not fenced
            continue
        if not fenced:
            out.append((i, line))
    return out


def numeric_units(text):
    """The card's numeric claims: each table (one unit) and each other prose line with a number a reader could hold
    us to (a decimal, a percentage, a ratio, a count with a unit)."""
    units, table = [], []
    for _, line in prose_lines(text):
        if line.startswith("|"):
            table.append(line)
            continue
        if table:
            units.append("\n".join(table))
            table = []
        if NUMERIC_CLAIM.search(line):
            units.append(line)
    if table:
        units.append("\n".join(table))
    return [u for u in units if NUMERIC_CLAIM.search(u)]


def unregistered_units(text, claims):
    """Numeric units no registered claim quote covers (a claim covers a unit when its quote is inside it)."""
    return [u for u in numeric_units(text) if not any(c["quote"] in u for c in claims)]


def table_rows(text, header):
    """The body rows of the table whose header line contains `header`, as lists of stripped cells."""
    unit = next(u for u in numeric_units(text) if header in u)
    return [[c.strip() for c in line.strip("|").split("|")] for line in unit.splitlines()[2:]]


QUESTION_TYPES = {"choice", "noul", "score", "box"}


def request_shape_errors(body):
    """The shape every decision request on the cards takes; a list of problems (empty when fine)."""
    errs = []
    if "state" not in body:
        errs.append("no state")
    qs = body.get("questions")
    if not isinstance(qs, dict) or not qs:
        return errs + ["questions must be a non-empty object"]
    for name, q in qs.items():
        t = q.get("type")
        if t not in QUESTION_TYPES:
            errs.append(f"{name}: unknown type {t!r}")
        if not isinstance(q.get("instructions"), str):
            errs.append(f"{name}: instructions must be text")
        c = q.get("criteria")
        if t == "choice" and not (isinstance(c, dict) and len(c) >= 2):
            errs.append(f"{name}: choice needs a criteria object with two or more options")
        if t == "score" and not (isinstance(c, list) and len(c) >= 2):
            errs.append(f"{name}: score needs a criteria list with two or more levels")
        if t == "noul" and c is not None and not (isinstance(c, dict) and set(c) <= {"true", "false"}):
            errs.append(f"{name}: noul criteria keys must be true/false")
    return errs


def response_shape_errors(resp, req):
    """The documented answer shape, checked against the request it answers."""
    errs = []
    answers = resp.get("answers")
    if not isinstance(answers, dict):
        return ["answers must be an object"]
    if set(answers) != set(req["questions"]):
        errs.append(f"answers {sorted(answers)} != questions {sorted(req['questions'])}")
    for name, a in answers.items():
        q = req["questions"].get(name, {})
        if a.get("type") != q.get("type"):
            errs.append(f"{name}: type {a.get('type')!r} != {q.get('type')!r}")
        if a.get("type") == "choice":
            p = a.get("probabilities", {})
            if set(p) != set(q["criteria"]):
                errs.append(f"{name}: probability keys {sorted(p)} != options {sorted(q['criteria'])}")
            if abs(sum(p.values()) - 1) > 1e-3:
                errs.append(f"{name}: probabilities sum to {sum(p.values())}")
            if p and a.get("choice") != max(p, key=p.get):
                errs.append(f"{name}: choice is not the most probable option")
        if a.get("type") == "noul" and not 0 <= a.get("noul", -1) <= 1:
            errs.append(f"{name}: noul out of [0, 1]")
        if a.get("type") == "score":
            p = a.get("probabilities", {})
            if len(p) != len(q["criteria"]):
                errs.append(f"{name}: {len(p)} levels != {len(q['criteria'])}")
            ev = sum(int(k) * v for k, v in p.items())
            if abs(ev - a.get("score", -1)) > 2e-3:
                errs.append(f"{name}: score {a.get('score')} != expected level {ev:.4f}")
    return errs


def numbers_close(shown, got, tol, path="$"):
    """Every number in `shown` (a card example) matches `got` within tol; every key shown must exist in `got`."""
    errs = []
    if isinstance(shown, dict):
        for k, v in shown.items():
            if not isinstance(got, dict) or k not in got:
                errs.append(f"{path}.{k} missing")
            else:
                errs += numbers_close(v, got[k], tol, f"{path}.{k}")
    elif isinstance(shown, list):
        if not isinstance(got, list) or len(shown) != len(got):
            errs.append(f"{path}: {got!r} does not match the shown list")
        else:
            for i, (a, b) in enumerate(zip(shown, got)):
                errs += numbers_close(a, b, tol, f"{path}[{i}]")
    elif isinstance(shown, (bool, str)):
        if shown != got:
            errs.append(f"{path}: {got!r} != {shown!r}")
    elif isinstance(shown, (int, float)):
        if not isinstance(got, (int, float)) or abs(got - shown) > tol:
            errs.append(f"{path}: {got!r} not within {tol} of {shown!r}")
    return errs


def argparse_flags(path):
    """Long flags a script declares with add_argument (one-line argparse included)."""
    import ast
    flags = set()
    with open(path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
        tree = ast.parse(f.read())
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Call) and getattr(node.func, "attr", "") == "add_argument":
            for a in node.args:
                if isinstance(a, ast.Constant) and isinstance(a.value, str) and a.value.startswith("--"):
                    flags.add(a.value)
    return flags


def command_flags(code, script_suffix):
    """For each command in a bash block that runs a script ending with script_suffix: the long flags passed to it."""
    import shlex
    out = []
    for cmd in re.split(r"\n(?=\S)", code.replace("\\\n", " ")):
        try:
            words = shlex.split(cmd, comments=True)
        except ValueError:
            continue
        for i, w in enumerate(words):
            if w.endswith(script_suffix):
                out.append([x.split("=")[0] for x in words[i + 1:] if x.startswith("--")])
    return out


def frontmatter(text):
    """The card's YAML front matter as a flat dict (lists for "- item" lines); enough for license/base_model/tags."""
    if not text.startswith("---\n"):
        return {}
    fm, key = {}, None
    for raw in text[4:text.index("\n---\n", 4)].splitlines():
        if raw.lstrip().startswith("- "):
            fm.setdefault(key, []).append(raw.split("- ", 1)[1].strip())
        elif ":" in raw:
            key, _, val = raw.partition(":")
            key = key.strip()
            fm[key] = val.strip() or []
    return fm


def referenced_files(text, prefix="", suffixes=(".py", ".sh", ".json", ".gguf", ".safetensors", ".jinja", ".png", ".md")):
    """Repo files the prose names in backticks (the card's download-folder prefix removed) and its relative links."""
    out = set()
    prose = "\n".join(t for _, t in prose_lines(text))
    for span in re.findall(r"`([^`\n]+)`", prose):
        s = span.strip()
        if " " in s or s.startswith(("-", "~", "/", "<", "\u2026")) or "\u2026" in s:
            continue
        if prefix and s.startswith(prefix):
            s = s[len(prefix):]
        if s.endswith(suffixes):
            out.add(s)
    for link in re.findall(r"\]\(([^)\s]+)\)", prose):
        if not re.match(r"^(https?:|#|mailto:)", link):
            out.add(link.split("#")[0])
    return sorted(p for p in out if p)


def bash_curls(text):
    """(block, url, body) for every curl -d example in the card's bash blocks."""
    return [(b, url, body) for b in readme_blocks(text) if b["lang"] == "bash"
            for url, body in curl_json_bodies(b["code"])]


def wait_post_200(url, body, seconds, proc=None, log=None, every=5):
    """POST body until 200 and return the answer; a server process (proc) that exits first fails at once, quoting
    the end of its log."""
    t0, last = time.time(), None
    while time.time() - t0 < seconds:
        if proc is not None and proc.poll() is not None:
            tail = open(log, "rb").read().decode("utf-8", "replace")[-2000:] if log else ""
            raise RuntimeError(f"the server process exited with {proc.returncode} before {url} answered:\n{tail}")
        try:
            code, resp = post_example(url, body, timeout=60)
            if code == 200:
                return resp
            last = (code, resp)
        except OSError as e:
            last = repr(e)
        time.sleep(every)
    raise TimeoutError(f"{url} never answered 200: last {last}")


def ports_free(*ports):
    """True when nothing listens on these loopback ports (so the card's server, not another, will answer)."""
    import socket
    for port in ports:
        with socket.socket() as sk:
            if sk.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", port)) == 0:
                return False
    return True


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- shared checks
def adapter_key_layout(path, prefix="base_model.model.model.language_model.layers."):
    """LoRA keys of a PEFT adapter against the module path the serving engine matches (vLLM's
    Qwen3_5ForConditionalGeneration: base_model.model.model.language_model.layers.N.*). An adapter saved from a
    text-only load carries base_model.model.model.layers.N.* and is accepted silently by vLLM 0.30 while applying
    nothing. Returns (total keys, keys outside the prefix); only the header is read."""
    hdr, _ = safetensors_keys(path)
    bad = [k for k in hdr if not k.startswith(prefix)]
    return len(hdr), bad


def probe_moves(base_top, other_top, eps):
    """A documented check must be able to fail: the top-5 logprob maps of two servings of the same probe prompt
    (the base model and the model under test) must differ by more than eps on the base's top token. Returns
    (top token, difference)."""
    top = max(base_top, key=base_top.get)
    diff = abs(float(base_top[top]) - float(other_top.get(top, -99.0)))
    return top, diff if diff > eps else -diff

        {%- set reasoning_content = reasoning_content|trim %}
        {%- if loop.index0 > ns.last_query_index %}
            {{- '<|im_start|>' + message.role + '\n<think>\n' + reasoning_content + '\n</think>\n\n' + content }}
        {%- else %}
            {{- '<|im_start|>' + message.role + '\n' + content }}
        {%- endif %}
        {%- if message.tool_calls and message.tool_calls is iterable and message.tool_calls is not mapping %}
            {%- for tool_call in message.tool_calls %}
                {%- if tool_call.function is defined %}
                    {%- set tool_call = tool_call.function %}
                {%- endif %}
                {%- if loop.first %}
                    {%- if content|trim %}
                        {{- '\n\n<tool_call>\n<function=' + tool_call.name + '>\n' }}
                    {%- else %}
                        {{- '<tool_call>\n<function=' + tool_call.name + '>\n' }}
                    {%- endif %}
                {%- else %}
                    {{- '\n<tool_call>\n<function=' + tool_call.name + '>\n' }}
                {%- endif %}
                {%- if tool_call.arguments is defined %}
                    {%- for args_name, args_value in tool_call.arguments|items %}
                        {{- '<parameter=' + args_name + '>\n' }}
                        {%- set args_value = args_value | tojson | safe if args_value is mapping or (args_value is sequence and args_value is not string) else args_value | string %}
                        {{- args_value }}
                        {{- '\n</parameter>\n' }}
                    {%- endfor %}
                {%- endif %}
                {{- '</function>\n</tool_call>' }}
            {%- endfor %}
        {%- endif %}
        {{- '<|im_end|>\n' }}
    {%- elif message.role == "tool" %}
        {%- if loop.previtem and loop.previtem.role != "tool" %}
            {{- '<|im_start|>user' }}
        {%- endif %}
        {{- '\n<tool_response>\n' }}
        {{- content }}
        {{- '\n</tool_response>' }}
        {%- if not loop.last and loop.nextitem.role != "tool" %}
            {{- '<|im_end|>\n' }}
        {%- elif loop.last %}
            {{- '<|im_end|>\n' }}
        {%- endif %}
    {%- else %}
        {{- raise_exception('Unexpected message role.') }}
    {%- endif %}
{%- endfor %}
{%- if add_generation_prompt %}
    {{- '<|im_start|>assistant\n' }}
    {%- if enable_thinking is defined and enable_thinking is false %}
        {{- '<think>\n\n</think>\n\n' }}
    {%- else %}
        {{- '<think>\n' }}
    {%- endif %}
{%- endif %}

FILE: h2o_lightning_shim.py

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""H2O-Lightning-4B shim: typed decisions over a stock vLLM server, one output token per decision. Stdlib only.

WHAT IT DOES
    POST /v1/systemone  (the decision wire format; /api/alpha/decisions is an alias)
    POST /v1/decisions  (OpenAI's Decisions API, e.g. client.decisions.create; mapped onto the same decision)
      -> renders each question in the prompt the model was trained on (serve_config.json: the chat template with
         thinking off, the system prompt, the "plain" layout, compact JSON for a structured state, one label per
         option, the "Answer:" prefill)
      -> sends ONE vLLM completion per question with max_tokens=1 and `logprob_token_ids` set to that question's
         label tokens, so every option is read at the answer slot and none can fall out of a top-k
      -> applies the temperature (one for every question type, or one per type: serve_config.json "temperature":
         {"choice": T, "noul": T, "score": T}), then the yes/no commit floor
      -> returns {"model", "answers", "probability_source", "effort_used", "usage"}

    Several questions about one state are sent concurrently; vLLM's prefix cache shares the state between them, and
    usage.input_tokens counts that shared head once plus each question's own tail.
    It generates no text and calls nothing but the local vLLM server.

    python3 h2o_lightning_shim.py --config serve_config.json --vllm http://127.0.0.1:8000 --port 8741

IMAGES (only when serve_config.json has an "image" block, and only for a request that carries an image)
    Accepted forms: a `data:image/...;base64,` URI or bare base64 image bytes anywhere inside `state` (a string, an
    object value, a list item); a chat-style image part inside `state` ({"type": "image_url", "image_url": {"url"}},
    {"type": "image", "image": ...}, {"type": "input_image", "image_url": ...}, {"type": "base64", "media_type",
    "data"}); the top-level fields `images`, `image`, `image_url`, `image_urls`, `image_data`, `image_base64` (data
    URIs, bare base64, http(s) URLs, or the part objects above); and multipart/form-data with the JSON in a
    `request` field and the images as file parts. A URL is an image only in those explicit places, never as a plain
    string inside `state`. Each image lifted out of `state` leaves `[image N]` in the record (the top-level images are
    numbered first). The question is then read through vLLM's chat completions with the images ahead of the same
    user text, the assistant turn pre-filled with the same "Answer:" (continue_final_message) and the same
    `logprob_token_ids` readout, so the prompt is the text prompt plus the image tokens. A request without an image
    takes the text path above, unchanged.

TWO-WAY READING (the recommended default; serve.sh TWO_WAY=1)
    The weights were trained to answer under two attention patterns: one-way (causal, the readout above) and two-way
    (bidirectional attention on the full-attention layers; the Gated DeltaNet layers stay causal). serve.sh serves
    two-way on ONE generate-mode engine of the vLLM build with per-request attention (github.com/h2oai/vllm, commit
    6bc6d5a839923abd64ab1f63a513258d74a5e16f; SHIM_TWO_WAY_FLAG=1): a two-way read carries vllm_xargs
    attention_mode=bidirectional and comes back through the same `logprob_token_ids` readout; the same engine answers
    one-way requests and Box questions. TWO_WAY=0 serves one-way only, on stock vLLM (the shim started with --vllm).
    A request chooses with `"attention_mode": "bidirectional"` or `"causal"` (also "two-way" / "one-way"); without it
    the deployment's default applies (two-way on the one engine; serve_config.json two_way.default /
    SHIM_ATTENTION_MODE). A request for a mode that is not running is a 400, never a silent answer in the other mode;
    every response states the mode it was read in. The two-way temperatures and yes/no floor are the block's own
    (two_way.temperature, two_way.noul_floor, two_way.image_temperature; the SHIM_* overrides apply to both modes); a
    missing one falls back to the one-way value with a WARNING line. Before the first two-way answer the shim reads
    three fixed probes flagged and unflagged: the flagged answers must be within two_way.probe_tolerance of the
    answers recorded on the release weights (two_way.probe_reference) and differ from the unflagged ones (an engine
    without the build ignores the flag); any failure is a 503, kept 10 s (SHIM_TWO_WAY_RETRY_SECONDS) before the
    engine is checked again. The earlier two-engine layout (a pooling-runner engine, --vllm-two-way, reading the final
    hidden state with label_rows.lightning and pooled_readout.py) is still accepted by the shim for a manual start but
    is no longer supported by serve.sh or the model card.

STATUS CODES (an evaluation runner stops after three consecutive failures unless a failure is a 422): a request this
system cannot answer -- too many options, a malformed question, a prompt over vLLM's context -- is a 422, scored as
one wrong answer; an empty request is a 400; an unreachable or failing vLLM is a 502, and a vLLM that does not serve
the configured model is a 503, so a broken backend stops a run; vLLM's own 401/403/429 pass through.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import base64
import binascii
import email.parser
import email.policy
import http.client
import ipaddress
import json
import logging
import math
import os
import re
import select
import socket
import ssl
import sys
import threading
import time
import urllib.parse
from concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer

SHIM_VERSION = "h2o-lightning-shim"
PRIMITIVES = ("choice", "noul", "score")
EFFORT_TIERS = ("fast", "balanced", "thorough")
# vLLM 0.30 accepts at most 128 `logprob_token_ids` per request; a question with more options is read in chunks
LOGPROB_CHUNK = int(os.environ.get("SHIM_LOGPROB_CHUNK", "128"))
TIMEOUT = float(os.environ.get("SHIM_TIMEOUT", "180"))
# vLLM's wording when a prompt does not fit --max-model-len (it has changed between releases)
_CONTEXT_ERROR = re.compile(r"maximum context length|maximum model length|max_model_len|too long and exceeds|"
                            r"longer than the maximum", re.I)
# Qwen3.5's chat template for a system and a user message with the generation prompt and thinking off; the template
# trims both contents (Jinja's `trim` is Python's str.strip)
CHATML = {"system": "<|im_start|>system\n{content}<|im_end|>\n",
          "user": "<|im_start|>user\n{content}<|im_end|>\n",
          "generation": "<|im_start|>assistant\n<think>\n\n</think>\n\n"}
# what the template writes for one image part, before vLLM expands the pad into the image's tokens
VISION_SLOT = "<|vision_start|><|image_pad|><|vision_end|>"
MAX_IMAGE_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("SHIM_MAX_IMAGE_BYTES", str(20 * 1024 * 1024)))
# A kept-alive connection to vLLM is reused only while the server still holds it open: vLLM (uvicorn) closes idle
# connections after 5 s, and a reused socket the server has closed can stall a large send (an image body) until the
# whole request timeout. Idle longer than LIGHTNING_BACKEND_IDLE_SECONDS, or closed by the server: a fresh connection.
IDLE_SECONDS = float(os.environ.get("LIGHTNING_BACKEND_IDLE_SECONDS", "3"))
# Sending a request (a few MB of images at most) never needs long; waiting for the answer may (a long read queued
# behind others), so only the send is bounded. A stalled send fails and is retried once on a fresh connection.
SEND_SECONDS = float(os.environ.get("LIGHTNING_BACKEND_SEND_SECONDS", "30"))


def _peer_closed(sock):
    """True when the server has closed (or written unexpectedly to) an idle kept-alive socket."""
    try:
        readable, _, _ = select.select([sock], [], [], 0)
    except (OSError, ValueError):
        return True
    return bool(readable)


class Unprocessable(ValueError):
    """A request in the contract's shape that this system cannot answer: a 422, one wrong item, the run goes on."""


class BadRequest(ValueError):
    """Not the contract's shape at all (no questions, an unknown effort tier): a 400."""


class UpstreamError(RuntimeError):
    """vLLM failed or could not be reached: a 502 (or 503, or vLLM's own 401/403/429), so a run stops."""

    def __init__(self, message, status=502):
        super().__init__(message)
        self.status = status


# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- the prompt
def render_state(state, compact):
    """The record: a string verbatim, anything else as JSON (compact when the config says so)."""
    if isinstance(state, str):
        return state
    if compact:
        return json.dumps(state, ensure_ascii=False, separators=(",", ":"))
    return json.dumps(state, indent=1)


def desc_text(v):
    """An option description or score level: a string verbatim, anything else as JSON."""
    return v if isinstance(v, str) else json.dumps(v, ensure_ascii=False)


def render_instr(value):
    """`instructions` may be a string, an object or an array."""
    return value if isinstance(value, str) else json.dumps(value, indent=1)


def options_of(question, max_options=255):
    """(option names, descriptions) for the three question types. A yes/no question is always [true, false]."""
    typ = question.get("type", "choice")
    criteria = question.get("criteria")
    if typ not in PRIMITIVES:
        raise Unprocessable(f"question type {typ!r} is not one of {list(PRIMITIVES)}")
    if typ == "noul":
        criteria = criteria or {}
        if not isinstance(criteria, dict):
            raise Unprocessable("noul criteria must be an object with true/false descriptions")
        true_desc = criteria.get("true", criteria.get("yes"))
        false_desc = criteria.get("false", criteria.get("no"))
        return ["true", "false"], [
            desc_text(true_desc) if true_desc not in (None, "") else "the statement holds",
            desc_text(false_desc) if false_desc not in (None, "") else "it does not",
        ]
    if typ == "score":
        if not isinstance(criteria, list) or len(criteria) < 1:
            raise Unprocessable("score criteria must be an ordered array of levels")
        return [str(i) for i in range(len(criteria))], [desc_text(value) for value in criteria]
    if not isinstance(criteria, dict) or len(criteria) < 1:
        raise Unprocessable("choice criteria must map option -> description")
    if len(criteria) > max_options:
        raise Unprocessable(f"choice supports at most {max_options} options")
    names = list(criteria)
    return names, [desc_text(criteria[name]) if criteria[name] is not None else name for name in names]


def option_lines(labels, names, descs):
    """One line per option: `A) name: description`."""
    if len(labels) < len(names):
        raise Unprocessable(f"{len(names)} options but only {len(labels)} labels")
    return [f"{l}) {n}: {d}" for l, n, d in zip(labels, names, descs)]


def user_turn(state_text, instructions, names, descs, labels, layout="plain"):
    """The user message."""
    body = "\n".join(option_lines(labels, names, descs))
    if layout == "plain":
        return f"record: {state_text}\nquestion: {instructions}\noptions:\n{body}"
    if layout == "markdown":
        return f"### RECORD\n{state_text}\n\n### QUESTION\n{instructions}\n\n### OPTIONS\n{body}\n"
    raise ValueError(f"layout {layout!r} is not supported")


def render_chat(system, user, prefill, chat=True):
    """The full prompt: the chat template for [system, user] with thinking off, then the answer prefill. Without the
    template, the same parts joined by blank lines."""
    if not chat:
        return "\n\n".join(([] if system is None else [system]) + [user]) + f"\n{prefill}"
    out = "" if system is None else CHATML["system"].replace("{content}", system.strip())
    return out + CHATML["user"].replace("{content}", user.strip()) + CHATML["generation"] + prefill


def chat_messages(system, user, prefill, image_urls):
    """The same prompt as render_chat, as chat messages for vLLM: the images ahead of the user text, and the assistant
    turn pre-filled with the prefill (sent with continue_final_message). Qwen3.5's template renders a final assistant
    turn after the last user turn as `<think>\\n\\n</think>\\n\\n` + its content, which is render_chat's generation
    prompt + prefill, so with no image the two token streams are the same (checked against vLLM at first use)."""
    msgs = [] if system is None else [{"role": "system", "content": system.strip()}]
    parts = [{"type": "image_url", "image_url": {"url": u}} for u in image_urls]
    msgs.append({"role": "user", "content": parts + [{"type": "text", "text": user.strip()}]})
    msgs.append({"role": "assistant", "content": prefill})
    return msgs


# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- images
IMAGE_KEYS = ("images", "image", "image_url", "image_urls", "image_data", "image_base64")
_DATA_URI = re.compile(r"data:(image/[A-Za-z0-9.+-]+);base64,(.*)", re.S | re.I)
_B64_CHARS = re.compile(r"[A-Za-z0-9+/=\s]+")
_B64_URLSAFE = re.compile(r"[A-Za-z0-9_\-=\s]+")
_SPACE = re.compile(r"\s")
_PART_TYPES = ("image_url", "image", "input_image")
# how base64 of a PNG, JPEG, GIF and WebP begins: the cheap test before anything inside `state` is decoded
_B64_HEADS = ("iVBORw0KGgo", "/9j/", "R0lGOD", "UklGR")


def sniff_image(raw):
    """The image type from its first bytes, or None."""
    if raw.startswith(b"\x89PNG\r\n\x1a\n"):
        return "image/png"
    if raw.startswith(b"\xff\xd8\xff"):
        return "image/jpeg"
    if raw.startswith((b"GIF87a", b"GIF89a")):
        return "image/gif"
    if raw[:4] == b"RIFF" and raw[8:12] == b"WEBP":
        return "image/webp"
    if raw.startswith(b"BM"):
        return "image/bmp"
    if raw[:4] in (b"II*\x00", b"MM\x00*"):
        return "image/tiff"
    return None


def _b64_bytes(s):
    """Decoded bytes of a (standard or URL-safe) base64 string, or None. The standard decoder checks the alphabet itself
    (validate=True), and whitespace is stripped only when there is some, so a large image is not scanned in Python."""
    t = re.sub(r"\s+", "", s) if _SPACE.search(s) else s
    if not t:
        return None
    try:
        return base64.b64decode(t + "=" * (-len(t) % 4), validate=True)
    except (binascii.Error, ValueError):
        pass
    try:
        if _B64_URLSAFE.fullmatch(t):
            return base64.urlsafe_b64decode(t + "=" * (-len(t) % 4))
    except (binascii.Error, ValueError):
        return None
    return None


def _data_uri(mime, raw):
    if len(raw) > MAX_IMAGE_BYTES:
        raise Unprocessable(f"an image of {len(raw)} bytes is over the {MAX_IMAGE_BYTES}-byte limit")
    return f"data:{mime};base64,{base64.b64encode(raw).decode('ascii')}"


# IMAGE URLS ARE FETCHED HERE, SAFELY, AND NEVER BY vLLM. An http(s) image URL in a request is downloaded by this shim
# and handed to vLLM as a data URI, so vLLM never makes a request on a caller's behalf (start it with
# --allowed-media-domains set to a name that never resolves, or with no egress, as a backstop). The rules:
#   - https only (LIGHTNING_IMAGE_URL_ALLOW_HTTP=true also allows http); no user:password in the URL; port 443 (80 for
#     http) unless the host is on LIGHTNING_IMAGE_URL_HOSTS
#   - every address the host resolves to must be public: loopback, private, link-local (169.254.0.0/16, the cloud
#     metadata address), CGNAT 100.64.0.0/10, multicast, reserved, IPv6 ULA / link-local / site-local and IPv4-mapped,
#     -compatible, -translated or embedded private addresses are refused; the connection goes to the checked address
#     (no DNS rebinding) and TLS still verifies the certificate for the host name
#   - at most 3 redirects, each one checked the same way; the final answer must be HTTP 200
#   - LIGHTNING_IMAGE_FETCH_SECONDS (5) is a hard deadline for the whole fetch (name lookup, connect, TLS, headers,
#     body and redirects): a watchdog closes the connection when it passes, however slowly the server sends
#   - LIGHTNING_IMAGE_FETCH_MAX_BYTES (20 MiB) per image, read with a cap; LIGHTNING_IMAGE_FETCH_MAX_TOTAL_BYTES
#     (4 x that) per request
#   - the links of a request are counted before any is fetched: more images than the deployment reads is refused
#     without fetching; at most LIGHTNING_IMAGE_FETCH_CONCURRENCY (8) fetches run at once in this process
#   - the response must say image/* and its bytes must be a PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, BMP or TIFF
#   - optional LIGHTNING_IMAGE_URL_HOSTS (allowlist) and LIGHTNING_IMAGE_URL_DENY_HOSTS (host names, compared in
#     their IDNA form); LIGHTNING_IMAGE_URLS=off refuses every image URL (locked-down deployments)
# The bytes live in memory only, for this request: no file, no cache, no log line with the URL or the image. Errors
# returned to the caller are generic (no address or fetch detail).
IMAGE_FETCH_SECONDS = float(os.environ.get("LIGHTNING_IMAGE_FETCH_SECONDS", "5"))
IMAGE_FETCH_MAX_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("LIGHTNING_IMAGE_FETCH_MAX_BYTES", str(20 * 1024 * 1024)))
IMAGE_FETCH_MAX_TOTAL_BYTES = int(os.environ.get("LIGHTNING_IMAGE_FETCH_MAX_TOTAL_BYTES", str(4 * IMAGE_FETCH_MAX_BYTES)))
IMAGE_FETCH_REDIRECTS = 3
_FETCH_SLOTS = threading.BoundedSemaphore(int(os.environ.get("LIGHTNING_IMAGE_FETCH_CONCURRENCY", "8")))
_FETCH_LOG = logging.getLogger("lightning.image_fetch")
_BLOCKED_V4 = [ipaddress.ip_network(n) for n in ("0.0.0.0/8", "10.0.0.0/8", "100.64.0.0/10", "127.0.0.0/8",
               "169.254.0.0/16", "172.16.0.0/12", "192.0.0.0/24", "192.0.2.0/24", "192.88.99.0/24", "192.168.0.0/16",
               "198.18.0.0/15", "198.51.100.0/24", "203.0.113.0/24", "224.0.0.0/4", "240.0.0.0/4")]
